Number of staff and nature of the first instance authority\n\n     Name in English              Number of staff          Ministry responsible            Is there any political\n                                                                                      interference possible by the\n                                                                                         responsible Minister with\n                                                                                          the decision making in\n                                                                                       individual cases by the first\n                                                                                            instance authority?\n                                 8,447 positions\n     Federal Office for                                     Federal Ministry of\n                                 (7,355 full-time\n      Migration and                                          the Interior and                    Yes     No\n                               positions in various\n     Refugees (BAMF)                                           Community\n                              asylum departments)\n\nSource: Information provided by the BAMF, up to date as of 15 December 2023.\n\nThe BAMF is responsible for examining applications for international protection and competent to take\ndecisions at first instance.\n\nThe BAMF has branch offices in all Federal States. As of February 2024, the BAMF website lists a total\nof 58 branch offices.33 The branch offices process the asylum procedures, but also carry out additional\ntasks (for instance, they function as contact points for authorities and organisations active in the\nintegration of foreign nationals, while some branch offices work exclusively on Dublin cases). Branch\noffices are assigned specific countries of origin, whereas the main countries of origin are processed in the\n\n\n\n33\n        BAMF, Locations, available at: https://bit.ly/3dFTd8w.\n\n\n                                                          23\n\nmajority of branch offices.34 In cooperation with the Federal States, the BAMF manages a distribution\nsystem for asylum seekers known as Initial Distribution of Asylum Seekers (Erstverteilung der\nAsylbegehrenden, EASY) system, which allocates places according to a quota system known as\n“Königsteiner Schlüssel” (see Asylum Act). The quota is based on the size and the economic strength of\nthe Federal States in which the centres are located. Furthermore, the system takes into account which\nbranch office of the BAMF deals with an asylum seeker's country of origin.\n\nAs of November 2023, the BAMF had 7.355 positions or “full-time job equivalents” working on various\naspects of asylum (meaning that the actual number of staff is much higher, since many of these positions\nare shared by people working part-time). Since the office is responsible for several other tasks on top of\nthe asylum procedure (e.g., research, integration, migration for reasons other than asylum and return\npolicies), not all staff members are working in the area of asylum. The overall number of positions is 8,138\naccording to the Federal Ministry of the Interior and Community and Community.35\n\nThe government provided the following numbers for positions in the relevant departments as of November\n2023:36\n        v asylum department (excluding revocation and Dublin procedures): 2,327.9 full-time\n          equivalents;\n        v revocation procedures: 105.2 full-time equivalents;\n        v procedures (appeal procedures, representation of the BAMF in court): 376.0 full-time\n          equivalents;\n        v quality management: 141.0 full-time equivalents;\n        v Dublin-procedures: 338.4 full-time equivalents.\n\nThe BAMF also has special officers for security issues. They are responsible for a whole range of issues\nand should be involved in asylum procedures whenever indications arise for instances of ‘extremism,\nterrorism, criminality, human trafficking, war crimes, crimes against humanity and smuggling of human\nbeings’.37 The special officers for security issues act as contact points between the BAMF and other\nauthorities, but they do not necessarily take part in interviews or take over responsibility of particular\nasylum procedures.\n\nQuality\n\nThe quality of BAMF asylum decisions has been much debated in recent years given the high number of\nappeals filed at the courts, but also because of “scandals” which prompted extensive media coverage in\n2017 and 2018.38 This was related, in part, to the high increase in personnel in 2015 and 2016 – likely\ndue to the spike in asylum applications –, accompanied by shortened training phases, with some decision-\nmakers not having received relevant training. As a result, the BAMF has undertaken several changes to\nthe training provided to decision-makers and to the quality assurance procedures since 2017. As of 2018,\nshort summaries of interview transcripts and notice letters are checked by a second employee. Randomly\nselected cases are subject to a more thorough quality control by the BAMF’s quality assurance division.\nIn addition, the BAMF also has a division for ‘Operative management of asylum procedures and\nintegration’ which ‘analyses developments and trends so that it is possible to recognise and react to a\nneed to act for management at an early date’, according to the BAMF.39 In particular, the decision-making\npractices of the different branch offices are monitored and branch offices with significant deviations from\n\n34\n      A list of all countries of origin and the allocated branch offices is available on the website of the Refugee\n      Council of Lower Saxony (up to date as of March 20221): https://bit.ly/3WJ0eg1.\n35\n      Federal Ministry of the Interior and Community, ‘Bundesamt für Migration und Flüchtlinge’, available in German\n      at: https://bit.ly/3qTH0qt.\n36\n      Federal Government, Response to information request by The Left, 20/9933, 28 December 2023, available in\n      German at: https://bit.ly/42NJDMa, 30-31.\n37\n      BAMF, Entscheiderbrief (newsletter for decision-makers) 4/2020, 1.\n38\n      For more detailed information, see AIDA, Country Report Germany - Update on the year 2019, July 2020,\n      available at: https://bit.ly/34so09M, 20-21.\n39\n      BAMF, Procedure management and quality assurance, 28 November 2018, available at: http://bit.ly/3DxsTgJ.\n\n\n                                                        24", "excerpt_sha256": "8e89c9fc9799b9e48de2bb9d933ba0310ce8d043d23aba81d6af8ac40819ba38", "verification_status": "corrected", "verification_notes": ["The excerpt does not support the claim that monitoring results were not automatically public."]}, {"evidence_id": "BATCH-0011-R03-E004", "source_id": "SRC-a007cee33f0d9205", "url": "https://asylumineurope.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/06/AIDA-DE_2023-Update.pdf", "title": "Country Report: Germany; 2023 Update", "publisher": "ECRE / AIDA", "raw_sha256": "35a16fff9b7b255d989c2a5cc6e56b8de7d67ce65e07a782935d6cac97244469", "fact_summary": "Federal guidance on identifying vulnerable asylum seekers was not legally binding, and the federal government did not systematically monitor whether the Länder implemented it; procedures varied considerably among arrival and reception centres.", "question_ids": ["3B.7", "3B.9", "4B.4", "6B.5", "8B.5"], "polarity": "adverse", "period_fit": "The finding was reported in 2023 and describes the institutional position during the target year.", "ruler_attribution": "The federal government retained guidance and oversight responsibilities, while the Länder controlled much reception-centre implementation.", "limitations": ["The source does not quantify the resulting harm across the population.", "The finding concerns federal-Länder administration rather than a personal directive by Scholz."], "start_unit": 94, "end_unit": 95, "locator": "page 94 through page 95", "exact_excerpt": "guidelines. However, the guidelines are not legally binding, and the government does not systematically\nmonitor the implementation of the guidelines in the Federal states.542\n\nIn Berlin, a ‘Network for persons with special protection needs’ has developed concepts for the\nidentification of vulnerable persons and their needs since 2008. The network, which refers to itself as a\nunique project in Germany, consists of seven NGOs which cooperate with the social services of the\nregional government. The NGOs have special expertise in the support of the following groups: traumatised\npersons and victims of torture; LGBTQI+; single women and pregnant women; children and\nunaccompanied children; persons with disabilities, with chronic diseases and older persons.543 The\nnetwork was involved in the development of guidelines for the social services to assist with the\nidentification of vulnerable groups.544 The guidelines, published in August 2018, provide detailed\ninformation on how vulnerable persons can be identified and on the determination of special support\nneeds. Social services at the arrival centre Berlin are instructed to systematically screen applicants for\nvulnerability in the reception procedure. If they find that an asylum seeker has special reception needs or\nrequires special procedural guarantees, they try to take appropriate measures (including appointments\nwith specialised institutions) and inform the BAMF and the State authority accordingly.545 In spite of these\nefforts, participating NGOs of the Berlin network have reported that measures to accelerate asylum\nprocedures in the ‘arrival centre’ have had a negative impact on the identification process, since the\ninterview in the asylum procedure is often scheduled before the persons concerned have a chance to\nspeak to staff members of NGOs or of the Federal State institutions.546 In practice when an asylum seeker\nneeds special procedural guarantees, the BAMF assigns ‘special officers’ for the interview (see Special\nprocedural guarantees).547 These officers are trained and experienced decision-makers on various groups\nof vulnerable people (e.g. unaccompanied minors, victims of human trafficking, traumatized persons,\nvictims of torture, gender-specific persecution. These officers shall guarantee that the necessary\nprocedural safeguards are adhered to. NGOs have criticised the fact that special procedural needs of\nasylum seekers are not considered (i.e., the lack of support and time to prepare for an interview).548 In\naddition, identification of a vulnerability by the social services does not entail a right to specific reception\nconditions, which can still be hard to obtain especially since social services and State authorities do not\nalways work hand in hand.549\n\nEspecially since the war in Ukraine has led to rising numbers of protection seekers, systematic\nshortcomings in the identification of vulnerabilities in arrival centres have been documented.550 Despite\nthe pledge for the establishment of a Pre-screening procedure for the identification of vulnerabilities by\n\n\n542\n      Federal Government, Response to information request by The Left, 20/7089, 31 May 2023, available in\n      German at: https://bit.ly/3SsRNpC; Bundesinitiative Schutz von geflüchteten Menschen in\n      Flüchtlingsunterkünften (Federal Initiative for the protection of asylum seekers/refugees in accommodation\n      centres’), BESAFE - Besondere Schutzbedarfe bei der Aufnahme von Geflüchteten erkennen, December\n      2022, available in German at: https://bit.ly/3Kf1jIZ.\n543\n      A list of the project partners of the „Berliner Netzwerk für besonders schutzbedürftige Flüchtlinge’ can be found\n      at: https://bit.ly/3dR5CGU.\n544\n      Leitfaden zur Identifizierung von besonders schutzbedürftigen Geflüchteten in Berlin. Für Mitarbeiter*innen\n      des Sozialdienstes des Landesamts für Flüchtlingsangelegenheiten (LAF), available in German at:\n      https://bit.ly/479wMo3.\n545\n      Flüchtlingsrat Berlin, Das Schnellverfahren für Asylsuchende im Ankunftszentrum Berlin, November 2017,\n      available in German at: http://bit.ly/2HdSDzb; See BafF, ‘Identifizierung besonderer Schutzbedürftigkeit am\n      Beispiel von Personen mit Traumafolgestörungen. Status quo in den Bundesländern, Modelle und\n      Herausforderungen’, June 2020, available in German at: https://bit.ly/3GsdrSm, 26.\n546\n      Nina Hager and Jenny Baron, ‘Verfahrensgarantien für psychisch Kranke oder Traumatisierte’, Asylmagazin\n      7–8/2017, available in German at: https://bit.ly/3TzhllY, 17-26, 22.\n547\n      Manuel Armbruster, Georg Classen und Katharina Stübinger, Neue Verfahrensabläufe im Ankunftszentrum\n      Berlin, Asylmagazin 10-11/2018, available in German at: https://bit.ly/3RsPnpf, 350.\n548\n      Manuel Armbruster, Georg Classen und Katharina Stübinger, ‘Neue Verfahrensabläufe im Ankunftszentrum\n      Berlin’, Asylmagazin 10-11/2018, available in German at: https://bit.ly/3RsPnpf, 350.\n549\n      See BafF, ‘Identifizierung besonderer Schutzbedürftigkeit am Beispiel von Personen mit\n      Traumafolgestörungen. Status quo in den Bundesländern, Modelle und Herausforderungen’, June 2020,\n      available in German at: https://bit.ly/3GsdrSm, 26.\n550\n      See e.g.: Refugee Council Berlin, Kein Ort für Schutzsuchende: Notunterkunft im Flughafen Tegel schließen,\n      13 March 2023, available in German at: https://bit.ly/3Ufa7Um.\n\n\n                                                         94\n\nthe Berlin senate in 2022,551 the Berlin Refugee Council criticises that this pre-screening mechanism has\nnever been implemented and that instead no specified reception conditions or other support mechanisms\nfor asylum seekers with special needs are in place in certain arrival centres.552\n\nIn Brandenburg, a questionnaire is handed out upon registration in the initial reception centre to detect\nvulnerabilities and possible psychological disorders. If the questionnaire indicates a potential vulnerability,\na screening interview takes place with the socio-psychological service of the Brandenburg immigration\nauthority (Zentrale Ausländerbehörde). Following the screening interview, if a vulnerability is detected\napplicants are referred to psychiatric counselling (which only takes place in Eisenhüttenstadt) and can be\nhoused in a special house for vulnerable applicants. The vulnerability is also communicated to the BAMF.\nHowever, in 2016 this was the case for under 1% of all asylum seekers, indicating that detection rates are\nvery low compared to the estimated prevalence of psychological distress among asylum seekers.\nFurthermore, the special accommodation houses both single men with psychological difficulties and single\nwomen who might have been victims of sexual violence.553 The Brandenburg Refugee Council criticises\nthat while there is a coordinated approach to identify vulnerabilities, the support measures vary depending\non the local governance. Accordingly, in some regions no specialised services for the accommodation of\nasylum seekers with special needs are available.554\n\nIn Lower Saxony, projects to improve the identification of vulnerable groups have been established in\nreception centres first in Friedland in 2012 and have since then expanded to all reception centres in the\nFederal State.555 Upon registration, all applicants are informed about special vulnerabilities during a\nmeeting with the social service of the reception centre and a further diagnosis is carried out in cases\nwhere there are indications of psychological disorders. While the authorities usually follow the\nrecommendations which follow from the diagnosis, between 2015 and 2017 only very few people were\nreferred for a diagnosis.556\n\nIn e.g., Rhineland-Palatinate,557 the regional government has adopted a protection concept which also\nincludes methods for the identification of vulnerabilities.558 This includes the following measures:\n     v Obligation to check for possible vulnerabilities in the reception centres during the initial stages of\n         the reception process and the asylum procedure;\n     v Intensification of communication between various actors and authorities involved in the reception\n         system and in the first steps of the asylum procedure;\n     v Documentation of possible vulnerabilities in a data system used by all authorities involved in the\n         reception process and in the asylum procedure;\n     v Training measures for persons employed by the Federal State in the reception centres to raise\n         awareness on the different forms of vulnerabilities.\n\n551\n      Berlin Senate, Senat beschließt Verfahren zur Unterstützung für besonders schutzbedürftige Geflüchtete aus\n      der Ukraine, 5 April 2022, available in German at: https://bit.ly/48NlJCt.\n552\n      Refugee Council Berlin, Kein Ort für Schutzsuchende: Notunterkunft im Flughafen Tegel schließen, 13 March\n      2023, available in German at: https://bit.ly/3Ufa7Um.\n553\n      See BafF, ‘Identifizierung besonderer Schutzbedürftigkeit am Beispiel von Personen mit\n      Traumafolgestörungen. 2023 Update", "publisher": "ECRE / AIDA", "raw_sha256": "35a16fff9b7b255d989c2a5cc6e56b8de7d67ce65e07a782935d6cac97244469", "fact_summary": "By June 2023, BAMF had 1,267 staff members serving as special officers for vulnerable applicants; their roles covered unaccompanied children, gender-specific persecution, trauma or torture, and trafficking, with 410 officers qualified for unaccompanied-children cases.", "question_ids": ["3B.2", "3B.7", "4B.5", "6B.4", "8B.4"], "polarity": "favorable", "period_fit": "Directly concerns staffing and institutional capacity in June 2023.", "ruler_attribution": "BAMF recruited, trained, and deployed the special officers under the federal government during Scholz's chancellorship.", "limitations": ["Some officers held multiple specialisations, so the category totals cannot be summed.", "The source does not show whether staffing was sufficient for workload or whether outcomes improved nationwide."], "start_unit": 98, "end_unit": 99, "locator": "page 98 through page 99", "exact_excerpt": "Moreover, young persons usually lose any entitlement to be supported in legal matters by the youth\nauthorities once they are declared to be adults in the course of the age assessment.574\n\nLatest numbers from 2021 show that 3,999 unaccompanied minors have been taken into custody. For\n1,608 of those this measure was ended due to an age assessment.575\n\n      2. Special procedural guarantees\n\n                                    Indicators: Special Procedural Guarantees\n\n      1. Are there special procedural arrangements/guarantees for vulnerable people?\n                                                           Yes        For certain categories      No\n             v If for certain categories, specify which: Unaccompanied children, traumatised persons\n                                                         Victims of torture or violence\n\n         2.1. Adequate support during the interview\n\nThe BAMF does not have specialised units dealing with vulnerable groups. According to the BAMF, all\ncase workers complete the EUAA training module on ‘Interviewing Vulnerable Persons’.576 If an applicant\nor a Federal State authority submits information to the BAMF that indicates vulnerability (such as medical\nrecords or information about specific physical, mental, intellectual or sensory impairments), such\ninformation is transferred to the case worker in charge who can decide to take measures such as allocate\nmore time for the interview, appoint an interpreter of a specific gender or allow the person to bring a\ntrusted person of their choice to the interview.577\n\nFor specific groups of vulnerable persons, the BAMF employs ‘special officers’ (Sonderbeauftragte)\nresponsible for interviews and decisions on claims by applicants with special needs. Special officers also\nadvise their colleagues in dealing with vulnerable applicants and are contact persons for specialised\ncounselling services and psycho-social centres.578 Staff members who become special officers must\ncomplete a training module for the specialisation they want to achieve. In addition, they follow the EASO\ntraining modules for their specialisation.579 Training covers both the identification and in the treatment of\nvulnerable persons.580 According to the BAMF, continuous training is offered for specific topics in the realm\nof the special officers’ responsibilities.581 The BAMF guidelines stipulate that the following cases shall be\nhandled in a particularly sensitive manner and, if necessary, by specially-trained decision-makers:582\n     v Unaccompanied children;\n     v Victims of gender-specific prosecution;\n     v Victims of human trafficking; and\n     v Victims of torture and traumatised asylum seekers.\n\nAs of June 2023, a total of 1,267 BAMF had one or more roles as special officers. This corresponds to\nroughly a third of full-time equivalent positions allocated to the first instance procedure (see Number of\n\n574\n        Nerea González Méndez de Vigo, Alterseinschätzung – ein Irrgarten ohne Ausweg? Rechtlicher Rahmen und\n        Verfahren der Alterseinschätzung in Deutschland, Asylmagazin 6-7/2019, available in German at:\n        https://bit.ly/2VI0w9G, 206-217.\n575\n        Statistisches Bundesamt, Statistiken der Kinder- und Jugendhilfe, Vorläufige Schutzmaßnahmen 2021, 27\n        July 2022, avialable in German at: https://bit.ly/3Sz6mru, 28.\n576\n        BAMF, Die Identifizierung vulnerabler Personen im Asylverfahren. Umsetzung in der Praxis des Bundesamtes\n        für Migration und Flüchtlinge, June 2022, available in German at: https://bit.ly/3Z7JHCU, 16.\n577\n        BAMF, Die Identifizierung vulnerabler Personen im Asylverfahren. Umsetzung in der Praxis des Bundesamtes\n        für Migration und Flüchtlinge, June 2022, available in German at: https://bit.ly/3Z7JHCU, 19.\n578\n        Information provided by the BAMF, 9 March 2023.\n579\n        BAMF, Die Identifizierung vulnerabler Personen im Asylverfahren. Umsetzung in der Praxis des Bundesamtes\n        für Migration und Flüchtlinge, June 2022, available in German at: https://bit.ly/3Z7JHCU, 16.\n580\n        BAMF, Die Identifizierung vulnerabler Personen im Asylverfahren. Umsetzung in der Praxis des Bundesamtes\n        für Migration und Flüchtlinge, June 2022, available in German at: https://bit.ly/3Z7JHCU, 16.\n581\n        Information provided by the BAMF, 9 March 2023.\n582\n        BAMF, DA-Asyl (Dienstanweisung Asylverfahren) – Belehrungen, 2010, 139.\n\n\n                                                       98\n\nstaff and nature of the first instance authority). The distribution among areas of responsibilities was the\nfollowing: Unaccompanied children (410), victims of gender-specific persecution (312) traumatised\npersons and victims of torture (291), victims of trafficking (254).583\n\nSpecially trained case officers may be included at all times of the asylum procedure, or take over, also\nprior to the interview, if vulnerabilitites are known. For example, if it becomes evident during the interview\nthat an asylum seeker belongs to one of these groups, the officer conducting the interview is obliged to\nconsult a special officer, in addition to notifying the reception centre if necessary and authorised by the\napplicant.584 A note on how the officers are planning to proceed must be added by the special officer to\nthe file, particularly if the special officer takes over the case as a result of their consultation. According to\ninformation provided by the government, there is an obligation in cases of unaccompanied minors for\nspecial officers to take over responsibility for the asylum procedures. In other cases of other vulnerable\ngroups, the specially trained case officer must be consulted and there are two options for further\nprocedures: either the special officer adopts an advisory role or they take over responsibility for the\nprocedure.585 However, the BAMF does not record the number of cases in which special officers are\nconsulted or in which procedures are handed over to special officers.586\n\nLawyers have reported that the introduction of special officers has led to some improvement in the\nhandling of ‘sensitive’ cases. The special officers receive special training in the area of LGBTIQ, they\nconduct interviews upon consultation by other BAMF officers and serve as multipliers for their\ncolleagues.587However, there have also been examples of cases in which indications of trauma and even\nexplicit references to torture did not lead to special officers being consulted.588 It has further been criticised\nthat there are shorcomings in the effective implementation of procedural guarantees for LGBTIQ+\npersons, which increases the risk of false decisions.589 There is no individual right to have a special officer\nhandling a person’s case, except for unaccompanied minors for whom this is mandatory. But if evidence\nsuggests that the person is vulnerable or if the person claims to have certain vulnerabilities, the interviewer\nis required to involve a special officer in the procedure, e.g. as consultant.590 However, the Administrative\nCourt of Berlin ruled that if special vulnerabilities have been detected, the absence of a special officer in\nthe asylum procedure constitutes a violation of procedural rights of vulnerable asylum seekers.591\n\n        2.2. Exemption from special procedures\n\nThe German Asylum Act exempts neither unaccompanied children nor persons with special procedural\nguarantees from the airport procedure, despite an express obligation under the recast APD to provide for\nsuch exemptions under certain conditions.592 It also makes no reference to ‘adequate support’ which\nshould be provided to those requiring special procedural guarantees.593\n\n\n\n\n583\n       The government notes that the figures cannot be added since some officers may have qualified in more than\n       one area; Federal Government, Reply to parliamentary question by The Left, 20/7503, 28 June 2023, available\n       in German at: https://bit.ly/3Sb12cF, 2-4.\n584\n       BAMF, Die Identifizierung vulnerabler Personen im Asylverfahren. In this preliminary stage children only have access to emergency psychological assistance, legal\nrepresentation and youth care services and are often not enrolled for school.620\n\nAfter the responsible youth welfare office has been determined, the regular taking into care procedure is\ninitiated. This procedure is subject to youth welfare law and analogous to the taking into care of youth in\nsituations where their welfare is in acute danger. It includes the appointment of a legal guardian by the\ncompetent Family Court and the so-called ‘clearing procedure’, which includes an examination of whether\nthere are alternatives to an asylum application, such as family reunification in a third country or the\napplication for a residence permit on humanitarian grounds.621\n\nThe guardian represents the minor in all legal matters and is the first contact point for all ‘proceedings\npertaining to asylum and residence law’, including the asylum procedure.622 The legal guardian has to file\nthe asylum application for the unaccompanied minor in written form to the responsible BAMF branch\noffice.623 The guardian acts as the minor’s legal representative, but also as a personal contact person with\nwhom unaccompanied minors can develop perspectives for the future and contribute to the assistance\nplanning procedure carried out by the youth welfare office.624 While the personal interview is conducted\nwith the minor themselves, the legal guardian is present during the interview and may ask them additional\nquestions (i.e. in case the minor forgot to mention an important aspect). They may also request to file\nstatements or explanations on behalf of the minor.625\n\nIn the majority of cases, the youth welfare office acts as guardian for the minor. Often, guardians appointed\nby the youth welfare offices are not in a position to sufficiently support the children in the asylum\nprocedure, because of overburdening, as some guardians in youth welfare offices are responsible for up\nto 50 minors at the same time.626 In 2023 it has been noted that the maximum number of 50 is not kept\n\n\n\n618\n      Gesetz zur Verbesserung der Unterbringung, Versorgung und Betreuung ausländischer Kinder und\n      Jugendlicher, Official Gazette I of 28 October 2015, 1802. Subsequent applications\n\n                                 Indicators: Subsequent Applications\n      1. Does the law provide for a specific procedure for subsequent applications?                   Yes      No\n\n      2. Is a removal order suspended during the examination of a first subsequent application?\n             v At first instance:          Yes             No\n             v At the appeal stage:        Yes             No\n\n      3. Is a removal order suspended during the examination of a second, third, subsequent application?\n              v At first instance:         Yes             No\n              v At the appeal stage:       Yes             No\n\nAs of 2024, some of the information in this section will be affected by the provisions of the new Act on the\nImprovement of Removals of 18 January 2024.\n\nThe law defines a subsequent application (Folgeantrag) as any claim which is submitted after a previous\napplication has been withdrawn or has been finally rejected.632 In case of a subsequent application the\nBAMF conducts a preliminary examination on the admissibility of the application. The admissibility test is\ndetermined by the requirements for resumption of procedures as listed in the Administrative Procedure\nAct.633 According to this, a new asylum procedure is only initiated if one of the following applies:\n    v The material or legal situation on which the decision was based has subsequently changed in\n         favour of the applicant;\n    v New evidence is produced which would have resulted in a more favourable decision for the\n         applicant in the earlier procedure; or\n\n\n\n\n627\n        Federal Association for Unaccompanied Refugee Minors (BumF), Es ist 5 nach 12: Rechtsverletzungen bei\n        unbegleiteten geflüchteten Kindern und Jugendlichen, 21 November 2023, available in German at:\n        https://bit.ly/3UiVX4x.\n628\n        Uta Rieger und Nerea González Méndez de Vigo, Kindgerechte Ausgestaltung des Asylverfahrens – Eine\n        Bestandsaufnahme, in: Deutsches Kinderhilfswerk e.V. Persons who enter Germany under the\nprogramme receive a residence permit for three years. The Federal State responsible for reception of the\npersons is to be determined according to the quota system for the distribution of asylum seekers (see\nRegistration of the asylum application), although family ties and other ‘criteria supporting integration’ are\nto be taken into account.776\n\nWhen announcing the programme, the Federal Government declared that ‘the new programme is now to\nbe implemented quickly’ and that it planned to approve around 1,000 requests per month, which is about\nthe amount of permissions granted in the months preceding the announcement. The programme is\nplanned to run until the end of the current government’s term in 2025.777 As of 30 June 2023, 229 persons\nhave been selected for admission.778 As of October 2023, only 13 of them had been admitted, due to the\npause of the admission procedure (see below).779 According to a press report, the NGOs Mission Lifeline\nand Luftbrücke Kabul alone have received around 32,000 requests as of early November 2022.780\n\nIn March 2023, the Federal Foreign office declared that all admission programs would be put to a halt for\nan indefinite time due to alleged abuse. A speaker of the\nForeigner’s Office declared that there were no evidence supporting a systemic misuse of the admission\nprograms.782 Only in one case a person who applied for admission has been identified as a possible\n‘threat’ (‘Gefährder’). Nevertheless, the Foreigners Office along with the Minister of Interior decided to\nintroduce additional screening mechanisms and to halt the admission program until the screening\nmechanism is in place.783 The screening procedure involves an automatic data reconciliation with\ndatabases from the national security agencies and interviews with the German domestic intelligence\nservice (Bundesamt für Verfassungsschutz), Federal Criminal Police Office (Bundeskriminalamt), the\nFederal Police or BAMF officers acting on behalf of these national security services.784 The admission\nprograms were restarted from 26 June 2023. In the first months of the newly introduced screening\nprocedure (26 June – 21 July 2023) 99 screening interviews took place, no security concerns have been\nidentified among those.785 The Federal government further states, that the capacities for the screening\nmechanism have been expanded since its start. Accordingly, as of August 2023 several hundreds of\nscreenings can be conducted per month.786\n\nThe Left party and NGOs such as PRO ASYL welcomed the launch of the programme but criticised that\n1,000 admissions per month was too low given the ‘real pressure of persecution’ for ‘people who have\nfought for democracy and human rights’. PRO ASYL further criticised that the relatively abstract selection\ncriteria could lead to an ‘ethically highly ambivalent protection lottery’787 especially in connection with the\nfact that only authorised agencies could put forward people and that the application is through an\nalgorithm-based IT application with little room to put forward specific individual circumstances.788 The\nNGO Kabul Luftbrücke reported problems with the IT application in October and November, leading to\ndelays in sending the online forms to authorities. A further point of critique is that the programme does not\nextend to persons who have managed to flee Afghanistan.789 Several NGOs also voiced concerns over\nthe practical implementation, demanding a better staffing of the counselling and coordination centres for\nthe programme and questioning the ‘organization and content’ of the procedure,790 especially given that\nit is required to have a passport in order to leave the country while obtaining one is made extremely\ndifficult by the Taliban government. One year after the official launch, in October 2023, several NGOs\nreflected on the development of the program. They demanded that the originally envisaged number of\n1,000 admissions per months should be fulfilled, that the procedure should be more transparent and that\nin additional admission schemes are necessary to meet the needs for protection.791\n\nIn addition to the Federal Government, several Federal States (Thuringia, Berlin, Hessen and Bremen)\nhave implemented admission programmes based on family ties to Afghans living in the respective Federal\nStates (for more information see Family Reunification). "SRC-a007cee33f0d9205", "url": "https://asylumineurope.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/06/AIDA-DE_2023-Update.pdf", "title": "Country Report: Germany; 2023 Update", "publisher": "ECRE / AIDA", "raw_sha256": "35a16fff9b7b255d989c2a5cc6e56b8de7d67ce65e07a782935d6cac97244469", "fact_summary": "In 2023 reception capacity was severely strained: Berlin and Hamburg were around 97% occupied, Berlin required 8,000 additional places, and overcrowded facilities produced reports of cold tents, illness, inadequate sanitation, lack of privacy, registration backlogs, insufficient health care, and violent conflicts; the federal government and states agreed on additional federal aid in November 2023.", "question_ids": ["3B.6", "3B.7", "3B.9", "5B.6", "5B.9", "6B.2", "6B.3", "6B.6", "8B.5", "8B.6", "8B.8", "8B.9"], "polarity": "mixed", "period_fit": "The capacity shortages, reported conditions, and November 2023 federal-state agreement concern 2023.", "ruler_attribution": "The report attributes the funding agreement to the federal government and federal states, while accommodation operations are primarily local and state responsibilities.", "limitations": ["The cited harms are drawn from multiple local reports and are not presented as a nationally standardized inspection result."], "start_unit": 151, "end_unit": 153, "locator": "page 151 through page 153", "exact_excerpt": "regulations provide for 6 or 7m² per person.971 A typical room in an initial reception centre has between 2\nand 4 beds, there are chairs and a table and each resident has a locker for themselves. Size of rooms\nmay vary, but rooms with a single bed are highly exceptional.\n\nMost initial reception centres have a policy to accommodate single women and families in separate\nbuildings or separate wings of their buildings. The AnKER centre in Manching/Ingolstadt for example\nprovides separate rooms for vulnerable persons.\n\nBath and toilet facilities usually consist of shower rooms and toilets which people have to share. As\nmentioned before, there are no regulations that oblige the Federal states to fulfil any standards. However,\nthe Federal Ministry for Family Affairs, Senior Citizens, Women and Youth and UNICEF have worked out\na policy paper stating minimum standards concerning the protection of refugees in reception centres.972\nThese guidelines state that sanitary facilities should be separate between genders and that those facilities\nshould be built in a way that guarantees privacy. Other guidelines recommend that one shower should be\navailable for 10 to 12 persons, but in some reception centres the ratio is worse than that, particularly in\nsituations of overcrowding. Cleaning of shared space (halls, corridors) as well as of sanitary facilities is\ncarried out by external companies in the initial reception centres.\n\nFood is supplied in the initial reception centres and is usually served in canteens on the premises of the\ncentres. In general, two or more menus are on offer for lunch and the management of the catering facilities\ntries to ensure that specific food is provided with regard to religious sentiments. Some, but not all initial\nreception centres also have shared kitchen space which enables asylum seekers to cook their own food;\nin AnkER centres, for instance, cooking is not allowed. Refrigerators for the use of asylum seekers are\navailable in some initial reception centres, but this seems to be the exception. In some centres, the\nmanagement does not allow hot water boilers for asylum seekers as this would be forbidden by fire\nregulations.973\n\nThe living conditions in many initial reception centres have been criticised by asylum seekers, volunteers\nand NGOs – especially in light of the extended obligatory stay in these facilities. In 2022 the conditions\ndeteriorated even more due to the massive overcrowding as consequence from the war in Ukraine and\nthe situation in Afghanistan. But still in 2023, small cities like Pulheim in North Rhine-Westphalia,974 as\nwell as middle size municipalities like Augsburg in Bavaria975 and Aachen in North Rhine-Westphalia976\nbut also big municipalities like Berlin977 and Hamburg978 face difficulties in accommodating new\nprotection seekers. In Berlin and Hamburg, around 97% percent of the reception capacities were\noccupied around October and November 2023.979\n\n\n\n\n971\n      European Migration Network, The Organisation of Reception Facilities for Asylum Seekers in Germany, 2013,\n      available in German at: https://bit.ly/48ojgO9, 26.\n972\n      Federal Ministry for Family Affairs, Senior Citizens, Women and Youth and UNICEF, Mindeststandards\n      zum Schutz von geflüchteten Menschen in Flüchtlingsunterkünften, available in German at:\n      https://bit.ly/48WAaV2.\n973\n      PROASYL and Refugee Council Berlin, Das Asylbewerberleistungsgesetz – Einschränkungen des\n      Grundrechts auf ein menschenwürdiges Existenzminimum für Geflüchtete, November 2022, available in\n      German at: https://bit.ly/3XrdSox, 100.\n974\n       WDR.de, Geflüchtete klagen über menschenunwürdige Zustände in Pulheimer Flüchtlingsunterkünften,\n      available at: https://bit.ly/3SKjme1.\n975\n      Augsburger Allgemeine, Augsburg gerät bei der Unterbringung von Geflüchteten wieder stärker unter Druck,\n      19 August 2023, available in German at: https://bit.ly/42MZ1Iq.\n976\n      InfoMigrants, ‘Refugees: German cities are reaching their limits’, 26 September 2022, available at:\n      https://bit.ly/3wpxjCp.\n977\n      Silke Mehring, Leichtbauhallen in Tegel geplant Unterbringung von Ukraine-Flüchtlingen in Berlin wird immer\n      komplizierter, rbb24, 02 December.2022, available in German at: http://bit.ly/3wdFPUT.\n978\n      Ibid.\n979\n      Rbb24.de, Berlin droht Verlust von tausenden Unterkunftsplätzen für Geflüchtete, 28 Novermber 2023,\n      available in German at: https://bit.ly/48nVWzN; NDR, Flüchtlingszahlen: Senat sieht Hamburg am Limit, 6\n      October 2023, available in German at: https://bit.ly/3SHfJ7w.\n\n\n                                                     151\n\nAccording to the administration of Berlin, 10,000 additional places were required in 2022.980 At the end\nof September 2023, the senate administration of Berlin spoke again of 8,000 more required places until\nthe end of 2023.981 Those necessary places are to be created in gymnasiums, hotels, exhibitions halls\nand through further usage of the old airport Tegel. In October 2023, the initial reception centre in Suhl,\nThuringia, was completely full and had imposed a freeze on admissions.982 However, the municipalities\ncannot offer any other accommodation facilities as all centres and apartments are full. The only possibility\nleft, according to the Thuringian Association of Towns and Municipalities, is the usage of sport halls.983\nMunicipalities in North Rine-Westphalia have started to rent hotels to provide accommodation for asylum\nseekers.984 Although this practice is very costly, it is preferred over blocking gymnasiums of local sport\nassociations. The authorities on the local, state and federal level blame each other for the shortcomings.\nWhile the local authorities are by law responsible for the accommodation of protection seekers,985 they\nclaim that the do not have enough financial and housing resources to fulfil the current need. They therefore\nask the Federal States to vacate more housing properties.986 The Federal States in turn urge the Federal\ngovernment to strengthen their efforts and to take up a coordinating role.987 After months of conflict, the\nFederal government and the Federal States have agreed in November 2023 – next to some deterrent\nmeasures that should decrease migration – upon more financial aid by the Federal Government.988\n\nAs consequence of the overcrowding, the authorities seem to be overburdened and deteriorating\nconditions have been reported. Under the law the state may derogate from the obligation to stay in initial\nreception centres in cases of overcrowding.989 Nevertheless, so far only Berlin has used this derogation\nclause and allows asylum seekers who have been allocated to Berlin under the “Köngisteiner Schlüssel”\nto live in private accommodations since the end of January 2023.990 In emergency shelters e.g. in Berlin,\nit has been reported that the tents at the former Berlin-Tegel airport do not protect from the cold causing\nnumerous illnesses and facilitating the spread of Covid-19 (on conditions in reception facilities during the\nCovid-19 pandemic see the 2021 Update).991 One partitioned area on such a tent is in general shared by\neight people. As a result, each asylum seeker has, according to calculations by the refugee council Berlin,\nonly about 2,6 m2 available for themselves.992 Additionally, since the airport is surrounded by barbed wire\nno systematic access for NGOs and volunteers is granted. At the same time protection seekers need to\ntake a shuttle bus to enter and exit the emergency shelter, thereby making it difficult for protection seekers\nto access legal aid and social assistance. In the emergency tents in Bremen protection seekers report of\nnon-functional and unclean sanitary facilities, coldness due to non-functional heating systems and a tense\natmosphere.993 Only one month later the municipality of Bremen decided to evacuate the tents due to the\n\n980\n      Thomas Frey, Ukraine-Ankunftszentrum wird um 3200 Plätze erweitert, Berliner Woche 06 January 2023,\n      available in German at: http://bit.ly/3CY9Jjy.\n981\n      Senatskanzlei Berlin, Mehr Plätze für die Unterbringung von Geflüchteten, press release from 26 September\n      2023, available in German at: https://bit.ly/3wnKSpd.\n982\n      Mdr.de, Erstaufnahmeeinrichtungen für Flüchtlinge – Was tun, wenn sie voll sind?, 6 October 2023, available\n      in German at: https://bit.ly/3I9iV74.\n983\n      Ibid.\n984\n      Wdr.de, Flüchtlinge in Kommunen: Letzter Ausweg Hotel, 26 January 2023, available in German at:\n      https://bit.ly/3UPlLFY.\n985\n      §44 AsylG in conjunction with the different Federal state’s Reception laws: e.g. §1 Landesaufnahmegesetz\n      Hesse; Inhabitants were partially relocated to emergency shelters on\nexhibition grounds. This solution was only temporary and local authorities have organised lightweight\nbuilding constructions which should serve until mid-2024.994 Even if asylum seekers do not live in tents\nbut in houses, the living conditions are in many cases catastrophic. In a reception centre in Pulheim,\nNorth Rhine-Westphalia, for example, the building smells heavily, the sanitary facilities are mouldy and\nrats run around the complex.995\n\nIn arrival centres, the overcrowding mostly leads to backlogs in the registration procedure and conflicts\namong the protection seekers stemming from the lack of privacy. Asylum seekers at the arrival centre in\nHamburg-Rahlstedt, for example, have reported inter alia a backlog of registration,996 lack of privacy,\nunclean sanitary facilities and disturbances at night. The sleeping areas are placed in former warehouses\nand divided by thin partitions into several compartments, which do not allow for privacy. Besides reading\nlamps attached to each bed, there is one common light for the whole warehouse, which is switched on\nfrom 8:00am to 22:00pm.997 A backlog of registration, lack of access to health care and social assistance\nhas been reported also from the arrival centre in Berlin.998 In the arrival centre in Thuringia, many violent\nconflicts have been reported stemming from the lack of staff members, stressed social workers and non-\ntrained security personnel.999 The backlog of registrations all over Germany has supposedly been caught\nup until the end of June 2023, according to the Federal Government.1000\n\nMore generally, studies published in 2020 have come to the conclusion that the accommodation in initial\nreception centres infringes upon children’s’ rights and constitutes a danger to their mental health. The\nspatial confinement, the experience of violence and removals, as well as the permanent uncertainty cause\npsychological stress and have a negative impact on children.1001 Health care and psychosocial support\nprovided for young refugees in the mass accommodations were described as worryingly inadequate for\nmost of the facilities.1002 The study of PROASYL and the Refugee Council Berlin support these findings.\nAccording to the study of 2022, especially the access to health care, access to adequate hygienic and\nother products such as strollers for toddlers are scarce.1003\n\nThe NGO ‘Ärzte der Welt’ (Doctors of the World) announced in September 2019 that an advice service\nrun by the organisation in the AnkER-centre of Manching/Ingolstadt was to be terminated. The NGO\ndescribed living conditions in the facility as ‘morbid’ and claimed that adequate treatment, in particular\ntreatment of persons with psychological disorders, was impossible under the circumstances. Insufficient\nprotection against assaults, lack of privacy and nocturnal disturbances were impeding the mental\nstabilisation of asylum-seekers at the facility and the NGO was no longer capable to bear responsibility\nfor the mental health of its patients. Moreover, the organisation claims that there was no system for the\nidentification of vulnerable persons in place at the facility.1004\n\n994\n       Weserreport, Geflüchtete ziehen um, 10 January According to the Federal State government, detainees in Pforzheim\ncan move around freely within the facilities’ accommodation and ‘leisure’ areas and are allowed to use\nmobile phones.1300 Two social workers are present in the facility, however de-facto only one was present\nover the latter half of 2023, according to the NGO Caritas, which provides external support to detainees\nthrough visits (see Access to detention facilities).1301 The Refugee Council of Baden-Württemberg\nhighlighted in 2019 that medical care had not always been guaranteed. For example, a priest had\norganised an urgent appointment at an ophthalmologist for a detainee, but the person concerned had not\nbeen allowed to leave the facility for this appointment.1302 According to Caritas, information on the\navailability of medical care cannot be verified as NGOs and support groups do not have contact to medical\nprofessionals working with detainees.1303 While the State government that took office in 2021 pledged\nsome improvements, they have only partially been adopted so far. By way of example, a “round table”\nwas set up in 2023 to exchange information between authorities in charge of detention and civil society.\nHowever, on the side of the civil society, no staff member who works in the detention facility is involved in\nthis discussion format, nor is the Refugee Council part of it. 1304 The promise to set up and make available\nseparate premises for full-time and voluntary staff and pastoral care has been only partially implemented\nthus far, according to Caritas: such a room is available in principle, but not yet fully operational as of\nJanuary 2024 due to questions of financing. When ready, the room will be used not only for independent\ncounselling but also for BAMF interviews in case of asylum requests filed during detention and for pastoral\ncare. 1305 No psychological support is available in the facility, Furthermore, as in many other detention\nfacilities in Germany, special rooms for detention with heightened security measures exist and detainees\nare placed there “too often”, e.g., if there is an assumed risk of suicide attempts before the planed\nremoval.1306\n\nHof, Bavaria: The detention centre in Hof opened on 26 October 2021 and has a capacity of 150 places.\nIt is administrated by the prison in Hof but separated from it ‘through structural and organisational\nmeasures’.1307 16 of the 150 places are foreseen for female detainees, and 4 places are suitable for\npersons with disabilities, according to the Ministry of Justice. As of October 2022, 54 law enforcement\nofficials and 20 social workers, psychologists, chaplains, and medical staff worked at the facility.1308\nAccording to the Ministry of justice, detainees have access to a range of leisure facilities including sports,\nand each room has a TV with access to international channels. Leisure activities are offered by the social\nservices present in the facilities. According to press reports, detainees can be outside their rooms between\n9 am and 7 pm.1309\n\nEichstätt, Bavaria: As of September 2023, the detention facility employs a total of 52 staff members, four\nof which are social workers and two psychologists.1310 Following a fact-finding mission conducted in April\n\n1300\n       State Ministry Baden-Württemberg, Lorek zu Besuch in Abschiebungshafteinrichtung Pforzheim, 22 July\n       2022, available in German at: https://bit.ly/4byGSCd.\n1301\n       Information provided by by the Caritasverband Karlsruhe e. Male and female quarters are separate. The female quarters are\nsupervised by female security guards only. The living units are divided into rooms, including single rooms\nand rooms with a number of beds. There are common showers, in which detainees also do their own\nlaundry. People are generally free to move within the facility, except during lunch and dinner. During lunch\n(starting 11:15 and until 13:00) and dinner, the men are locked in their rooms (a head count also takes\nplace during dinner). Women are not locked in their rooms.\n\nReports about self-harm are frequent, usually to prevent removal. Tensions were frequent but have\nreduced since the opening of additional detention facilities in Bavaria in 2018. Disciplinary measures can\nbe taken if a person violates rules e.g., withdrawal of shopping rights, access to television etc. in\naccordance with prison rules. Detainees can also be isolated for a certain period of time, for their own\nsafety. However, where isolation is used, it is for very short periods of time.1311\n\nIn a report published in May 2019, the European Committe for the Prevention of Torture (CPT)\nsummarised detention conditions at Eichstätt as follows (based on a visit to the facility in August 2018):\n\n        ‘While material conditions at the facility in Eichstätt were generally very good in terms of state of\n        repair, living space, access to natural light, ventilation and equipment, the environment did not\n        take into account the specific situation of immigration detainees, with a number of restrictions that\n        appeared unnecessary.[…].\n        Moreover, due to the applicable legislation on the execution of prison sentences, the regime for\n        immigration detainees held at the establishment was – to all intents and purposes – comparable\n        to that of sentenced prisoners. The only significant differences concerned the fact that the\n        detainees were not obliged to work and that they could usually have more contact with the outside\n        world and spend more time outside their cells. However, male detainees – in contrast to female\n        detainees – did not benefit from an open-door regime (indoors); [...].’1312\n\nAccording to the CPT‘s report, common rooms with sports equipment or television were only accessible\nfor a maximum of two and a half hours per day, while the outdoor exercise yard could only be accessed\nin the afternoon. While detainees were allowed to make phone-calls and were provided wih free-of-charge\nphone cards for that purpose, they did not have access to the internet. Persons who behaved violently or\nwho had either attempted or threatened to commit suicide can be referred to security cells at the Eichstätt\nfacility. The facility‘s director stated that persons were not referred to these cells for disciplinary reasons,\nbut only if the pose a risk to themselves or to others. The CPT criticised that conditions in the security\ncells were ‘akin to solitary confinement’, since people were locked up for 24 hours a day without access\nto outdoor exercise and they often were not allowed to make phone calls or receive visits.1313 On 24\nNovember 2022, the court of appeal of Coburg issued a ruling according to which a detention order was\nunlawful based on the detention conditions in Eichstätt, which are considered too similar to prisons so\nthat the detention centre does not meet the standards of a “specialised detention facility” as defined by\nthe CJEU in its decision of 10 March 2022 (see Place of detention).1314 Following the decisions, the Green\nopposition demanded to end detention in Eichstätt, while the Federal State government claims that\nseveral changes have been implemented in response to the ruling, such as an extension of visiting times\nand allowing detainees to wear their own clothes, and that the conditions do meet the standards of the\nEU Return Directive as set out in the CJEU’s ruling.1315\n\n1311\n       ECRE, The AnkER centres Implications for asylum procedures, reception and return, April 2019, available at:\n "Dunkel\nZEIT Bühne / Olaf Scholz\n:\n\"Nicht ankleben, sondern anpacken\"\nIhr Browser unterstützt die Wiedergabe von Audio Dateien nicht. Download der Datei als mp3: https://zeitonline.simplecastaudio.com/579b89cc-b402-41a6-a73b-5624fe676f45/episodes/8fd4a0db-b6fb-43bb-a1cf-a56df8a36920/audio/128/default.mp3?awCollectionId=579b89cc-b402-41a6-a73b-5624fe676f45&awEpisodeId=8fd4a0db-b6fb-43bb-a1cf-a56df8a36920\n1 Std. 32 Min.\nAbonnieren\nPodcast abonnieren\nPodcast bei Spotify\nPodcast bei Apple Podcasts\nPodcast bei Deezer\nPodcast als RSS-Feed\nAlle Folgen\nBundeskanzler Olaf Scholz spricht über die Letzte Generation, den Ampel-Streit und das Umfragehoch der AfD. Und über den schwersten Moment seines politischen Lebens.\nVon\nMariam Lau\nund\nRoman Pletter\nAus der Serie\n:\nZEIT Bühne\n5. Juni 2023, 15:41 Uhr\n9\nKommentare\nSie können sich Artikel der ZEIT in der Google-Suche nun bevorzugt anzeigen lassen.\n\nJetzt aktivieren\nMehr erfahren\nAusblenden\nHinweis ausblenden\nEine Stunde ZEIT mit Olaf Scholz\n© Phil Dera für DIE ZEIT\n\"Der Stillstand ist überwunden\", sagt Scholz im Gespräch mit ZEIT-Politikredakteurin Mariam Lau und Wirtschaftsressortleiter Roman Pletter.\nOlaf Scholz\nsieht sich als Klimakanzler. Aber wie will er den Klimaschutz weiter vorantreiben und wie kann er seine Koalition wieder zusammenführen? Man habe beim Klimaschutz ein riesiges Tempo vorgelegt, sagte Scholz. Die Forderungen der\nLetzten Generation\nkritisiert der Bundeskanzler als unzureichend und zu wenig inhaltlich: \"Nicht ankleben, sondern anpacken.\" Man könne mit ihm beispielsweise darüber streiten, ob man noch mehr Offshore-Windparks bauen sollte. Die Aktivistinnen und Aktivisten wollten aber nur hören, dass bald \"alles vorbei\" sei und man das nun anerkennen müsse – das sei \"angesichts der Tatsache, dass die Welt ein konkreter Ort ist, nicht konkret genug\".\nDer Frage, wie ein perfektes\nHeizungsgesetz\nfür ihn aussehe, weicht Scholz im Gespräch aus. Das werde er dann sagen, wenn das Gesetz fertig verhandelt sei. Weitere Verzögerungen beim Klimaschutz dürfe es nicht geben: In Vorgängerregierungen seien immer wieder langfristige Klimaschutzziele ausgegeben, aber kurzfristig nichts getan worden. Das habe sich nun geändert. \"Es quietscht ab und zu, aber wir haben die Kurve noch gekriegt\", so Scholz.\nAuf die Frage, wie er sich den jüngsten\nUmfrageerfolg der AfD\nerkläre, sagte Scholz, wir lebten in einer Zeit der Umbrüche, in der sich viele Menschen ihrer Zukunft nicht sicher seien. Das schaffe Raum für Parteien, die \"schlecht gelaunt das Vergangene loben\". Dagegen müsse man eine Zukunft setzen, an die man glauben könne, so Scholz.\nAuf die Frage, was der politisch schwerste Moment seines Lebens war, antwortet der Kanzler: \"Das ist schwer zu sagen, aber ganz sicherlich die G20-Krawalle in Hamburg.\"\nDas Gespräch ist eine Aufzeichnung der Veranstaltung \"Lange Nacht der ZEIT 2023\", die am 3. Juni in Hamburg vor Publikum stattfand und per\nLivestream\nübertragen wurde.\nSeitennavigation\nStartseite\nKommentieren\nJetzt teilen auf:", "excerpt_sha256": "e2e59b5c61129ad0d465323f684783dc34fdd7fe6c6ea8133edcd7b3dc3d6dec", "verification_status": "accepted", "verification_notes": []}, {"evidence_id": "BATCH-0013-E002", "source_id": "SRC-1ece7afdc08027fd", "url": "https://www.gmfus.org/download/article/20955", "title": "An Early Assessment of Germany’s Zeitenwende in Security Policy", "publisher": "German Marshall Fund of the United States", "raw_sha256": "216e8007e9573de274a06103f04ca45d7f9c0b4d8399412beaf403465a6d493b", "fact_summary": "A German Marshall Fund assessment reported that Scholz’s Zeitenwende included a €100 billion special security fund intended to meet or exceed NATO’s 2% defense-spending target, purchases of F-35 aircraft, military modernization, increased deployments in several NATO states, and military, financial, and humanitarian support for Ukraine; it stated that implementation remained uncertain and that weapons deliveries had been delayed by arms unavailability, bureaucratic procedures, concerns about Russian gas cutoffs, and political caution.", "question_ids": ["2B.5", "2B.8", "8B.2", "8B.3", "8B.6", "8B.7"], "polarity": "mixed", "period_fit": "The source is from 2022 and describes policies initiated under Scholz that remained relevant to the 2023 security posture.", "ruler_attribution": "The assessment attributes the announced commitments and policy direction to Scholz and the German government.", "limitations": ["The source predates the target period and does not establish the extent of 2023 execution.", "The assessment is an external analysis and does not provide a complete audit of spending or delivery outcomes."], "start_unit": 1, "end_unit": 2, "locator": "extracted HTML block 1 through extracted HTML block 2", "exact_excerpt": "Download Article | German Marshall Fund of the United States\nSkip to main content\nGo Back\nAn Early Assessment of Germany’s Zeitenwende in Security Policy\nApril 12, 2022\nBy\nJames (J.D.) Bindenagel\nJust three days after President Vladimir Putin sent Russia’s army to invade Ukraine, Chancellor Olaf Scholz shed Germany’s reluctant leadership and dovish foreign policy.\nSpeaking in the Bundestag, he called on the country to invest much more in its security to protect its freedom and democracy. Scholz’s words amounted to an earthquake, upending decades of settled German policy. The change was reminiscent of Ernest Hemingway’s description of how bankruptcy happens: slowly at first and then suddenly. Under what has been labelled as the\nZeitenwende\n—a turning-point awakening—the impossibility of German leadership now appears possible.\nThe announced new role for Germany in deterring further military aggression in Europe while ending Putin’s war sets the stage for a new national security strategy debate in the country. Moreover, the commitment to provide Ukraine with military, financial, and humanitarian support to defend its freedom, sovereignty, and democracy expands Germany’s role beyond NATO.\nPutin’s decision to invade Ukraine has destroyed the international security order that has provided peace in Europe since the end of the Second World War. As a result, Germany adopted unprecedented sanctions on Russia in concert with the EU and the United States. These have cut off Russian banks and businesses from financing, excluded some banks from the SWIFT banking communication system, targeted oligarchs, and enacted other measures to isolate and punish Moscow for its military aggression. These measures reversed decades of Germany appeasing and accommodating Russia.\nScholz also reversed Germany’s free-riding approach within NATO. His new measures include the creation of a €100 billion special fund to invest in security capabilities and meet or exceed the NATO defense-spending target of 2 percent of GDP. Germany’s decision to buy US F-35 fighters also lays to rest the debate on its participation in the alliance’s nuclear-sharing agreement. The purchase of armed drones will modernize military capabilities. The Bundeswehr has increased deployments in Lithuania, Romania, Slovakia, and the Baltic states. Moreover, Germany will strive for energy independence from Russia by creating new domestic energy sources while it weans itself off its Russian supply.\nIn the aftermath of the invasion, Germany’s policy of not supplying arms to conflict zones ended. It is now sending thousands of antitank and antiaircraft weapons to Ukraine. Nevertheless, it is under massive political pressure—not only from Kyiv but also from the media and from its allies—to do more to support the country in its struggle, especially as more war crimes reveal the brutality of Russian troops.\nAlthough the Zeitenwende signals a new German policy, implementing the changes it entails remains uncertain.\nScholz has promised more weapons for Ukraine after delivering 2,700 Soviet-era, shoulder-fired Strela missiles and other antitank weapons as well as agreeing to its allies releasing German-made weapons to the country. The list of possible deliveries of German weapons include 58 infantry fighting vehicles from former East Germany stocks now located in the Czech Republic and high-tech weapons. In addition, Ukrainians continue to ask for more weapons, including Leopard main battle tanks, Marder infantry fighting vehicles, 2,000 self-propelled howitzers, and Cobra artillery-detection systems from the Bundeswehr’s stocks.\nProviding weapons that could kill Russians “is very difficult to stomach for many Germans,” as one expert\nputs it\n. However, with murderous Russian attacks on Ukrainians that will very likely culminate on the anniversary of the defeat of Nazi Germany on May 9, Foreign Minister Annalena Baerbock has spoken out in favor of supplying heavy weapons to Ukraine. She\nsaid\n: “What is clear is that Ukraine needs further military material, especially heavy weapons.” Ukraine’s defense of its sovereignty is its right under the UN Charter, Chapter VII, Article 51. Furthermore, German support for other countries’ self-defense is allowed under the German constitution.\nAlthough the\nZeitenwende\nsignals a\n\nnew German policy\n, implementing the changes it entails remains uncertain. For example, reasons for the delays in shipments of German weapons to Ukraine keep flowing from Berlin, such as concern that Russia will cut off gas supplies or bureaucratic procedures. Defense Minister Christine Lambrecht has argued that arms are not available from her ministry. But Baerbock has\nstated\n: “Now is not a time for excuses; now is a time for creativity and pragmatism.” In the chancellor’s office, the question is “What’s the hurry?”\nScholz may have ushered in an era of monumental change, but does this really mean that Germany will be leaving behind its unique form of restraint in foreign and security policy? The country’s ugly legacy of military aggression during the 20th century produced a deep-seated mindset that views dialogue and multilateralism as the key and, often, the only tool of foreign policy. The question today is whether Germany will move away from this tradition and add its weight to the security of Europe.\nRelated Reading: Zeitenwende—The Dawn of the Deterrence Era in Germany\nGermany’s foreign policy took a dramatic turn on Sunday, February 27, 2022. In a special address to the Bundestag, Chancellor Olaf Scholz announced measures that are being called “a revolution.”\nRead More\nFollow Us!\nDon’t forget to subscribe to our ideas on transatlantic issues via:\nNewsletter\nGet the latest! A trois, c’est encore plus compliqué. Les tensions qui traversent\nla coalition « feu tricolore »\nau pouvoir en Allemagne le confirment. Quinze mois après son élection, le chancelier social-démocrate Olaf Scholz (SPD) peine à donner un cap clair à sa politique, tiraillé entre les positions de son parti et celles de ses partenaires écologistes et libéraux (FDP), de plus en plus difficiles à concilier.\nSigne, le plus récent, de ces divergences : le report de la présentation en conseil des ministres du projet de budget pour 2024. Prévue mercredi 15 mars, elle a été différée sine die, faute d’accord entre le ministre des finances, Christian Lindner (FDP), qui se veut le garant vétilleux de l’équilibre des comptes publics, et ses collègues du SPD et des Verts, dont les exigences se révèlent particulièrement coûteuses.\nC’est le cas du ministre de la défense, Boris Pistorius (SPD), qui demande pour son prochain budget 10 milliards d’euros de plus que les 50 milliards d’euros prévus jusqu’à présent pour 2024. It established an integrated-security approach covering defense, civil protection, diplomacy, policing, development, cyber-security, supply-chain resilience, energy and raw-material diversification, climate risks, biodiversity, food security, and pandemic prevention; Scholz described the strategy as the starting point for a continuous cross-government process.", "question_ids": ["1B.10", "1B.9", "3B.9", "5B.9", "6B.6", "8B.1", "8B.2", "8B.5", "8B.6", "8B.8"], "polarity": "favorable", "period_fit": "Directly within 2023-2023.", "ruler_attribution": "Scholz presented the cabinet-adopted strategy with four ministers and described its purpose and continuing implementation process.", "limitations": ["The source documents adoption and stated institutional design, but not completed implementation, funding adequacy, or measurable outcomes by year-end."], "start_unit": 6, "end_unit": 9, "locator": "extracted HTML block 6 through extracted HTML block 9", "exact_excerpt": "Zum Mastodon-Kanal der Bundesregierung\nZum YouTube-Kanal der Bundesregierung\nNewsletter-Anmeldung der Bundesregierung\nFolgen Sie dem Bundeskanzler:\nZum Instagram-Account des Bundeskanzlers\nZum TikTok-Kanal des Bundeskanzlers\nZum X-Kanal des Bundeskanzlers\nKontakt\nErklärung zur Barrierefreiheit\nBarriere melden\nGebärdensprache\nLeichte Sprache\nNavigations-Hinweise\nEnglish\nFrançais\nReformen für Deutschland\nAktuelles\nSchwerpunkte\nBundeskanzler\nService\nAktuelles\nNationale Sicherheitsstrategie vorgestellt\nIntegrierte Sicherheit für Deutschland\nEin verändertes sicherheitspolitisches Umfeld, eine zunehmende Multipolarität, Bedrohungen für die Demokratie, Gesellschaft und Wirtschaft und die Auswirkungen der Klimakrise. Das Kabinett hat die Nationale Sicherheitsstrategie beschlossen – als Antwort auf diese Herausforderungen und zum Schutz Deutschlands und seiner Bürgerinnen und Bürger.\nMittwoch, 14. Juni 2023\n\n4 Min. Lesedauer\nper E-Mail teilen, Integrierte Sicherheit für Deutschland\nper Facebook teilen, Integrierte Sicherheit für Deutschland\nBeitrag teilen\nE-Mail\nper E-Mail teilen, Integrierte Sicherheit für Deutschland\nFacebook\nper Facebook teilen, Integrierte Sicherheit für Deutschland\nThreema\nper Threema teilen, Integrierte Sicherheit für Deutschland\nWhatsApp\nper Whatsapp teilen, Integrierte Sicherheit für Deutschland\nX\nper X teilen, Integrierte Sicherheit für Deutschland\nLink kopieren\nDie Nationale Sicherheitsstrategie beantwortet viele Fragen: Wer bedroht unser Land und wie? Und wer kümmert sich darum das zu verhindern?\nGrafik: Bundesregierung\n„Zum ersten Mal in der Geschichte unseres Landes haben wir eine Nationale Sicherheitsstrategie für die Bundesrepublik Deutschland erarbeitet“, sagte Bundeskanzler Olaf Scholz. Gemeinsam mit Außenministerin Annalena Baerbock, Bundesfinanzminister Christian Lindner, Bundesinnenministerin Nancy Faeser und Verteidigungsminister Boris Pistorius stellte der Kanzler nach der Kabinettssitzung die nun beschlossene Strategie vor.\nStark veränderte Rahmenbedingungen\n„Was sich früher als Weißbuch allein auf die Verteidigungspolitik beschränkte, folgt nun einem viel umfassenderen, systematischen Gesamtansatz“, so Scholz weiter. Bereits im Koalitionsvertrag der regierungsbildenden Parteien enthalten, sei die Erarbeitung der Nationalen Sicherheitsstrategie in den vergangenen 18 Monaten mit massiven sicherheitspolitischen Rahmenbedingungen konfrontiert und von ihnen beeinflusst worden.\nDer Auftrag zur Erarbeitung ging an das Auswärtige Amt – „vor der Zeitenwende, vor dem russischen Überfall auf die Ukraine, vor dem brutalen Angriffskrieg, der seither in der Ukraine tobt, und vor den Explosionen der Nordstream Pipelines“, so der Kanzler. Bei allen Veränderungen bleibe es die zentrale Aufgabe des Staates, ohne Abstriche für die Sicherheit seiner Bürgerinnen und Bürger zu sorgen. „Denn: Ohne Sicherheit gibt es keine Freiheit, keine Stabilität und auch keinen Wohlstand.“\nAußenministerin Baerbock sagte, der russische Angriffskrieg gegen die Ukraine habe gezeigt, „dass Frieden und Freiheit nicht vom Himmel fallen“. Die Herausforderungen für die Sicherheit Deutschlands zögen sich durch alle Lebensbereiche. Dies gelte etwa für Medikamente genauso wie für den\nCyber\n-Raum oder die Sauberkeit von Wasser.\nDie Sicherheit Deutschlands brauche eine „360-Grad-Perspektive“, sagte Bundesfinanzminister Lindner. Dazu leisten alle Ressorts der Bundesregierung einen Beitrag. Auch die Finanzpolitik sei hier gefordert, so Lindner. Die Corona-Pandemie und der Energiepreisschock des vergangenen Jahres wären unter anderen Umständen mit einer erheblichen Gefährdung des sozialen Friedens verbunden gewesen, „wenn wir nicht über die fiskalischen Reserven verfügt hätten“. Zum anderen hänge mit der internationalen Finanzstabilität unmittelbar Krisenprävention zusammen.\n\nDrei zentrale Dimensionen des politischen Handelns\n„Wehrhaft. Resilient. Nachhaltig. Integrierte Sicherheit für Deutschland“ – unter diesem Titel steht die Nationale Sicherheitsstrategie.\nWehrhaftigkeit\nOberste Aufgabe der Sicherheitspolitik ist sicherzustellen, dass die Bürgerinnen und Bürger in Deutschland auch künftig in Frieden, in Freiheit und Sicherheit leben können. Dafür muss Deutschland sich und seine Verbündeten schützen und verteidigen können.\nDazu bekennt sich die Bundesregierung weiter unverrückbar zu\nNATO\nund\nEU\n. Die Bundeswehr wird nachhaltig gestärkt, um ihren Kernauftrag – die Landes- und Bündnisverteidigung – zu gewährleisten. Durch einen gesamtgesellschaftlichen Ansatz werden die Zivilverteidigung und der Bevölkerungsschutz gestärkt.\nUnverändert bekennt sich die Bundesregierung zur Verantwortung im internationalen Krisenmanagement. Denn die Sicherheit Deutschlands ist untrennbar verbunden mit der Sicherheit und Stabilität anderer Weltregionen.\nResilienz\nEin Leben in Freiheit kann nur gewährleistet werden, wenn Gesellschaft und Volkswirtschaft widerstandsfähig, anpassungsfähig und im Inneren gefestigt sind. Deshalb muss die freiheitlich-demokratische Grundordnung gegen illegitime Einflussnahme von außen geschützt und verteidigt werden.\nGemeinsam mit den internationalen Partnern tritt die Bundesregierung ein für eine freie internationale Ordnung auf Grundlage des Völkerrechts und der Charta der Vereinten Nationen. Ziel ist es auch, einseitige Abhängigkeiten in der Rohstoff- und Energieversorgung durch Diversifizierung der Lieferbeziehung zu reduzieren.\nDie Bundesregierung wird Rohstoffprojekte gemeinsam mit der Wirtschaft gezielt fördern und strategische Reserven anlegen. Außerdem wird sie die Cyber-Sicherheit stärken und auch der Weltraumsicherheit mehr Aufmerksamkeit widmen.\nNachhaltigkeit\nEine fundamentale Bedrohung – insbesondere für die kommenden Generationen – erwächst aus den Folgen der Klimakrise und der Bedrohung der natürlichen Lebensgrundlagen.\nNicht nur bei der Bekämpfung der Klima-, Biodiversität- und Ökosystemkrise, der Stärkung der globalen Ernährungssicherheit und der globalen Pandemieprävention gilt daher für die Bundesregierung das Primat der Prävention: frühzeitiges Handeln rettet Leben.\nWeitere Informationen finden Sie auch auf der Website zur\nNationalen Sicherheitstrategie\n. Das Dokument im Wortlaut finden Sie hier\nzum Nachlesen\n.\nKanzler Scholz (2.v.l.) und die Ministerinnen und Minister Lindner, Baerbock, Faeser und Pistorius (v.l.) auf dem Weg in die Bundespressekonferenz.\nFoto: Bundesregierung/Denzel\nIntegrierte Sicherheit ist mehr als Verteidigung\n\nDie Nationale Sicherheitsstrategie zieht Schlüsse für die Zukunft, um die Sicherheit zu gewährleisten. Dafür begründet sie eine Politik der Integrierten Sicherheit. „Es geht nicht allein um Verteidigung und Bundeswehr“, erläuterte Kanzler Scholz. Es gehe vielmehr „um die ganze Palette unserer Sicherheit – über Diplomatie genauso wie Polizei, Feuerwehr und Technische Hilfswerke, über die Entwicklungs-Zusammenarbeit, über\nCyber\n-Sicherheit und über die Resilienz von Lieferketten.“\nDie Strategie, so Scholz, sei nicht „Endpunkt, sondern Ausgangspunkt“ für einen kontinuierlichen Prozess, bei dem alle staatlichen Ebenen für eine dauerhafte Stärkung der Sicherheit zusammenwirken – aber auch Wirtschaft und Gesellschaft. „Deutschland ist ein starkes Land. Deshalb können wir die Aufgaben, die vor uns liegen, voller Zuversicht und Vertrauen angehen.“\nDie Nationale Sicherheitsstrategie, so Außenministerin Baerbock, richtet sich nicht nur an die Bundesregierung, sondern an die gesamte Gesellschaft. „Die Sicherheitsstrategie wird nur funktionieren, wenn wir sie europäisch und transatlantisch verankern“. Nötig sei eine viel engere Zusammenarbeit mit Partnern auf anderen Kontinenten.\nNach der Nationalen Sicherheitsstrategie hat die Bundesregierung am 13. Juli 2023 erstmals eine ressortübergreifende und umfassende\nChina-Strategie\nbeschlossen. Zentrales Ziel der Strategie ist es, den komplexen Beziehungen zu China eine Struktur und eine Richtung zu geben – in vollem Einklang mit den Zielsetzungen der\nEU\n.\nBeitrag teilen\nper E-Mail
